\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U|U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T|1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{-1}|1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U|U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T|1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{-1}|1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U|U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{-1}|1}

Figure 4.2.
The
H
-function and
DA
-bimodule of the positively clasped Whitehead link
\mathrm{Wh}_{+}
.

The normalized Alexander polynomial
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mathrm{Wh}}(x_{1},x_{2})
is

\widetilde{\Delta}_{\mathrm{Wh}}(x_{1},x_{2})=-x_{1}x_{2}+x_{1}+x_{2}-1,\text{ with linking number }\ell=0.

See Figure
4.2
for the
H
-function and the
DA
-bimodule of the positively clasped Whitehead link. Each box represents a generator
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{2i}
.

Each staircase
\mathcal{C}_{t}
for
t\neq 0
consists of a single generator. For the staircase
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{0})=-2H(0,1)=0,\,\,\,

\displaystyle\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{0})=-2H(0,1)-0-2=-2,

\displaystyle\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{2})=-2H(0,-1)=-2,\,\,\,

\displaystyle\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{2})=-2H(0,-1)-0-(-2)=0,

\displaystyle\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{1})=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{2})+1=-1,\,\,\,

\displaystyle\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{1})=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{0})+1=-1.

The pattern
P
is an unknot, so
\mathcal{S}
consists of a single generator
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}
, with

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0})=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0})=0.

The actions
L_{a}
for
a=W,Z,\sigma,\tau
are computed using the fact that they are non-zero on homology, and using the grading shifts formulas in Equation (
4.1
). The arrow labeled
(W,Z)|Z
from
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{0}
to
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{1}^{0}
represents a non-trivial
\delta^{1}_{3}
-action
\delta_{3}^{1}(W,Z,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{0})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{1}^{0}\otimes Z
, which is a chain homotopy between

L_{W}(L_{Z}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{0}))=\delta_{2}^{1}(W,\delta_{2}^{1}(Z,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{0}))=\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{2}\otimes Z^{2}\text{ and }\delta_{2}^{1}(WZ,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{0})=\delta_{2}^{1}(U,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{0})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{0}\otimes U,

and similarly for the arrow labeled
(Z,W)|W
from
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{2}
to
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{0}_{1}
. The actions of the form
\delta_{2}^{1}(a,-)
or
\delta_{3}^{1}(a,b,-)
are determined by the above maps. Furthermore, the resulting maps can be chosen to be
U
-equivariant. See
[
5
]
*Section 5.5 for the details.

4.2.
On the
H
-function of two-component L-space links

In this section, we study the
H
-function of two-component L-space links. In particular, we prove some stabilization properties of the
H
-function in the horizontal and vertical directions, refining the corresponding statement in Lemma
2.6
. The main result is Lemma
4.9
. We mostly focus on the case where
L
is of the form
L=L_{P}
for an L-space satellite operator
P
. Equivalently, we focus on L-space links
L=L_{1}\cup L_{2}
when
L_{1}
is an unknot.

In the horizontal direction, recall the following definition in
[
5
]
*Section 10:

Definition 4.5
.

If
L
is a two-component L-space link with linking number
\ell
, we define the
width
of
L
, denoted by
N_{L}
, to be

N_{L}:=\min\left\{t_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}+\ell/2\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}L_{\sigma}:\mathcal{C}_{t}\to\mathcal{S},\text{ and }L_{\tau}:\mathcal{C}_{-t}\to\mathcal{S}\text{ are }\\
\text{homotopy equivalence for all }t\geq t_{0}\end{array}\right\}.

Sometimes we write
N
instead of
N_{L}
if there is no danger of confusion. For most of the paper, we will focus on the two-component link
L=L_{P}
associated to the satellite operation with pattern
P
, and we will call
N_{L_{P}}
the
width
of the pattern
P
.

We recall that the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
is constructed using the
H
-function of
L
. Using the symmetry of the
H
-function in Lemma
2.6
and the stabilization behavior of the
H
-function as
t\to\infty
, we observe that the quantity
N_{L}
satisfies the following equations:

(4.2)

\begin{split}N_{L}=&\min\left\{t_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}+\ell/2\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}H_{L}(t,r)=H_{L}(t+1,r),\text{ for}\\
\text{all }t\geq t_{0}\textrm{ and }r\in\mathbb{Z}+\ell/2\end{array}\right\}\\
=-&\max\left\{t_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}+\ell/2\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}H_{L}(t-1,r)=H_{L}(t,r)+1,\\
\text{for all }t\leq t_{0}\textrm{ and }r\in\mathbb{Z}+\ell/2\end{array}\right\}.\end{split}

The following lemma gives a bound on
N_{L}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Suppose
L=L_{1}\cup L_{2}
is a two-component L-space link with linking number
\ell
, and width
N_{L}
. Then,

-N_{L}+g_{3}(L_{1})\leq\frac{\ell}{2}\leq N_{L}-g_{3}(L_{1}).

In particular, if
L_{1}
is an unknot, then

-N_{L}\leq\frac{\ell}{2}\leq N_{L}.

Proof.

This follows from the stabilization behavior

H_{L_{1}}(t-\ell/2)=H_{L}(t,\infty)

in Lemma
2.6
, together with the following property of the
H
-function of an L-space knot
K
: If
g=g_{3}(K)
is the Seifert genus of
K
, then

H_{K}(g-1)=H_{K}(g)+1=1,\quad H_{K}(-g+1)=H_{K}(-g)=g.

It follows that

\begin{split}H_{L}(g+\ell/2-1,\infty)&=H_{L}(g+\ell/2,\infty)+1,\\
H_{L}(-g+\ell/2+1,\infty)&=H_{L}(-g+\ell/2,\infty).\end{split}

Therefore, from Equation (
4.2
), we have

N_{L}\geq g+\ell/2,\text{ and }-N_{L}\leq-g+\ell/2.

∎

Remark 4.7
.

When
L=L_{1}\cup L_{2}
is a two-component L-space link such that
L_{1}
is an unknot, which is the case for
L_{P}=\mu\cup P
, it follows from the relation between the
H
-function and the normalized Alexander polynomial
\widetilde{\Delta}_{L}
in Proposition
2.5
that:

(1)

If
\widetilde{\Delta}_{L}(x_{1},x_{2})=0,
then
N_{L}=0
.

(2)

If
\widetilde{\Delta}_{L}(x_{1},x_{2})\neq 0
, then
N_{L}
is the highest power of
x_{1}
appearing in
\widetilde{\Delta}_{L}
.

In the vertical direction, it is helpful to make the following definition involving the
H
-function:

Definition 4.8
.

If
L
is a two-component L-space link with linking number
\ell
and
H
-function
H_{L}(t,r)
, we set

R_{t}:=\max\left\{r\in\mathbb{Z}+\ell/2\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}H_{L}(t,r+1)=H_{L}(t,r)\text{ and}\\
H_{L}(t,r-1)=H_{L}(t,r)+1\end{array}\right\}.

Note that
R_{t}
is equal to the
A_{2}
-grading of the top generator
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{0}
in the staircase
\mathcal{C}_{t}
that we used in our construction of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
in Section
4.1
. We denoted this earlier by
r^{t}_{0}
. It follows easily from the definition of
r^{t}_{0}
that

H_{L}(t,r)=H_{L}(t,\infty)=H_{L_{1}}\left(t-\ell/2\right),\text{ for all $t\in\mathbb{Z}+\ell/2$ and $r\geq R_{t}$.}

We now study how the numbers
R_{t}
vary as
t
changes, when
L_{P}=\mu\cup P
is a two-component L-space link such that
\mu
is an unknot:

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
P
be an L-space pattern and let
L_{P}=\mu\cup P
be the associated two-component link in
S^{3}
. Write
\ell=\mathrm{lk}(\mu,P)
for the linking number,
g_{3}(P)
for the Seifert genus of
P
viewed as a knot in
S^{3}
, and
N=N_{L_{P}}
for the width of
L_{P}
. Then the numbers
R_{t}
satisfy the following:

(1)

When
t\leq-N
,
R_{t}=g_{3}(P)-\frac{\ell}{2}
.

(2)

When
t\leq\frac{\ell}{2},

R_{t-1}\leq R_{t}
.

(3)

When
t\geq\frac{\ell}{2}
,
R_{t}\geq R_{t+1}
.

(4)

When
t\geq N
,
R_{t}=g_{3}(P)+\frac{\ell}{2}
.

In words,
R_{t}
is constant for
t\leq-N
, then non-decreasing until
t=\frac{\ell}{2}
, then non-increasing, and finally becomes constant for
t\geq N
.

In particular, the maximum of
R_{t}
is achieved when
t=\frac{\ell}{2}
, and

R_{t}\geq g_{3}(P)-\frac{\ell}{2}\text{ for }t\leq\frac{\ell}{2},\qquad R_{t}\geq g_{3}(P)+\frac{\ell}{2}\text{ for }t\geq\frac{\ell}{2}.

See Figure
4.3
for an illustration of the shape of
R_{t}
in the case of the Mazur pattern, where
\ell=1
and
g_{3}(P)=0
. We add a box to the position corresponding to the top generator
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{0}
in each staircase
\mathcal{C}_{t}
.

\begin{array}[]{|c|ccccccccc|}\hline\cr\hbox{\diagbox[]{{\shortstack[l]{$r$}}}{{\shortstack[r]{$t$}}}}&\cdots&\nicefrac{{-5}}{{2}}&\nicefrac{{-3}}{{2}}&\nicefrac{{-1}}{{2}}&\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}&\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}&\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}&\cdots&\\
\hline\cr\vdots&\ddots&3&2&1&0&0&0&\reflectbox{$\ddots$}&\\
\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}&\cdots&3&2&1&\boxed{0}&0&0&\cdots&\\
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}&\cdots&3&2&\boxed{1}&1&\boxed{0}&\boxed{0}&\cdots&\\
\nicefrac{{-1}}{{2}}&\cdots&\boxed{3}&\boxed{2}&2&1&1&1&\cdots&\\
\nicefrac{{-3}}{{2}}&\cdots&4&3&2&2&2&2&\cdots&\\
\vdots&\reflectbox{$\ddots$}&5&4&3&3&3&3&\ddots&\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Figure 4.3.
The
H
-function and the Alexander gradings of the top generator
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{0}
in each
\mathcal{C}_{t}
for
t\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}
.

Proof.

The statement for
t\leq-N
and
N\leq t
follows from the the description of
N
in terms of
H
-function in Equation (
4.2
). Therefore it suffices to prove the second and third claims, which concern monotonicity of
R_{t}
.

We first point out that since
\mu
is the unknot, we have

(4.3)

H_{L}(t,R_{t})=H_{L}(t,\infty)=H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)=\begin{cases}\frac{\ell}{2}-t&\text{if $t<\frac{\ell}{2},$}\\
0&\text{if $t\geq\frac{\ell}{2}$.}\end{cases}

We now prove Claim (
2
). Suppose, for contradiction, that
R_{t_{0}-1}>R_{t_{0}}
for some
t_{0}\leq\frac{\ell}{2}
. Equation (
4.3
) implies that if
t\leq\frac{\ell}{2}
, then

(4.4)

H_{L}(t,R_{t})=\frac{\ell}{2}-t\quad\text{and}\quad H_{L}(t-1,R_{t-1})=\frac{\ell}{2}-t+1.

In particular, by the monotonicity of
H_{L}(t,r)
in
r
and by Equation (
4.4
), we have

H_{L}(t_{0}-1,R_{t_{0}})\geq H_{L}(t_{0}-1,R_{t_{0}-1}-1)=H_{L}(t_{0}-1,R_{t_{0}-1})+1.

This implies

H_{L}(t_{0}-1,R_{t_{0}})-H_{L}(t_{0},R_{t_{0}})\geq H_{L}(t_{0}-1,R_{t_{0}-1})+1-H_{L}(t_{0},R_{t_{0}})=2,

which contradicts the bounded gap property of
H_{L}
. See the left figure in Figure
4.4
for an illustration.

We now prove Claim (
3
). When
t\geq\frac{\ell}{2}
, we have

H_{L}(t,R_{t})=H_{L}(t,\infty)=0,\text{ and }H_{L}(t+1,R_{t+1})=H_{L}(t,\infty)=0.

Suppose, for contradiction, that
R_{t_{0}}<R_{t_{0}+1}
for some
t_{0}\geq\frac{\ell}{2}
, then we have

H_{L}(t_{0}+1,R_{t_{0}})\geq H_{L}(t_{0}+1,R_{t_{0}+1}-1)=H_{L}(t_{0}+1,R_{t_{0}+1})+1=1,

by the monotonicity of
H_{L}(t,r)
in
r
and the definition of
R_{t_{0}+1}
. However, this would imply
H_{L}(t_{0}+1,R_{t_{0}})>H_{L}(t_{0},R_{t_{0}})
since

H_{L}(t_{0}+1,R_{t_{0}})\geq H_{L}(t_{0}+1,R_{t_{0}+1})+1=1>H_{L}(t_{0},R_{t_{0}})=0,

contradicting to the monotonicity of
H_{L}(t,r)
in
t
. See the right figure in Figure
4.4
for an illustration. This completes the proof. ∎

\begin{array}[]{|c|cc|}\hline\cr\hbox{\diagbox[]{{\shortstack[l]{$r$}}}{{\shortstack[r]{$t$}}}}&t_{0}-1&t_{0}\\
\hline\cr R_{t_{0}-1}&k&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
R_{t_{0}}&\geq k+1&k-1\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{|c|cc|}\hline\cr\hbox{\diagbox[]{{\shortstack[l]{$r$}}}{{\shortstack[r]{$t$}}}}&t_{0}&t_{0}+1\\
\hline\cr R_{t_{0}+1}&\vdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
R_{t_{0}}&0&\geq 1\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Figure 4.4.
The left figure depicts the contradiction when
t\leq\frac{\ell}{2}
, where
k=\frac{\ell}{2}-(t-1)
. The right figure depicts the contradiction when
t\geq\frac{\ell}{2}
.

4.3.
The bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}

In this section, we study the type-
AA
bimodule

{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}:={}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\boxtimes{}_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\mathbb{F}[Z]_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}.

This bimodule will typically have
m_{0|1|0}
,
m_{1|1|0}
,
m_{2|1|0}
and
m_{0|1|1}
non-zero. In this section, we prove that this bimodule is homotopy equivalent to a type-
AA
bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
which has only
m_{1|1|0}
and
m_{0|1|1}
non-trivial, and we describe this bimodule.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is a (positive) staircase complex, viewed as a type-
D
structure over
\mathbb{F}[W,Z]
. Write
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0},\dots,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{m}
for the generators of
\mathcal{C}
. Write
\delta_{1}^{1}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i+1})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i}\otimes W^{\alpha_{i}}+\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i+2}\otimes Z^{\beta_{i}}
. When we set
W=0
, the effect is to replace
\mathcal{C}
with the type-
D
structure shown below:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2m-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2m}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\alpha_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\alpha_{m}}}

If we tensor the above complex (viewed as a type-
D
module over
\mathbb{F}[Z]
) with
{}_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\mathbb{F}[Z]_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, then
\mathcal{C}\boxtimes\mathbb{F}[Z]
is quasi-isomorphic to the type-
A
module

(4.5)

\mathcal{C}^{\diamond}:=\mathbb{F}[Z]\oplus\mathbb{F}[Z]/Z^{\alpha_{1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{F}[Z]/Z^{\alpha_{m}},

where we view the above as having vanishing differential and the obvious action of
\mathbb{F}[Z]
.

We observe that there is a strong deformation retraction of chain complexes over
\mathbb{F}

(4.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\boxtimes\mathbb{F}[Z]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}

The map
\Pi
is projection of modules, the map
I
is the inclusion of
\mathbb{F}
-vector spaces. The map
H
vanishes on
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}Z^{i}
as well as
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2j+1}Z^{i}
for all
i
and
j
. Furthermore,

H(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i}Z^{j})=\begin{cases}\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i-1}Z^{j-1}&\text{ if }j\geq\alpha_{j}\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

We apply the above construction to the modules
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
. These bimodules are built from staircases
\mathcal{C}_{t}
for
t\in\ell/2+\mathbb{Z}
(in idempotent 0) and a staircase
\mathcal{S}
(in idempotent 1). We define a right
\mathbb{F}[Z]
module

\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}

by replacing each copy of
\mathcal{C}_{t}
and
\mathcal{S}
with
\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\diamond}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}
, respectively. The strong deformation retraction in Equation (
4.6
) can be applied to each copy of
\mathcal{C}_{t}
and
\mathcal{S}
separately, to give a strong deformation retraction of chain complexes over
\bm{\mathbf{I}}
between
\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\boxtimes{}_{\mathbb{F}[X]}\mathbb{F}[Z]
and
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
. Applying the homological perturbation lemma for type-
AA
bimodules, Lemma
3.2
, equips
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
with an
AA
-bimodule structure over
(\mathcal{K},\mathbb{F}[Z])
which is homotopy equivalent to
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
and which has vanishing
m_{0|1|0}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

The only non-vanishing actions
m_{i|1|j}
on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
are
m_{1|1|0}
and
m_{0|1|1}
. Furthermore, the right action of
\mathbb{F}[Z]
sends
\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\diamond}
to itself, and coincides with the obvious
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module structure from its description in Equation (
4.5
). Similarly, the right
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module structure sends
\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}\otimes T^{i}
to itself, and coincides with the natural analogous action.

Proof.

We note that
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
admits an algebraic grading supported in
\{0,1\}
. We also view
\mathbb{F}[Z]
as being supported in algebraic grading 0. The generators
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i}^{t}
are in algebraic grading 0, and the generators
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i+1}^{t}
are in algebraic grading
1
. We view
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
as having an algebraic grading supported in grading 0. The maps
\Pi
and
I
preserve the algebraic grading, while
H
increases it by 1. The action of
\delta_{2}^{1}
of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
preserves the algebraic grading, whereas the action
\delta_{3}^{1}
increases it by 1. Since
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
is supported in algebraic grading 0, by examining the structure maps constructed by the homological perturbation lemma (see Equation (
3.1
)) it follows immediately that the only actions on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
which are non-trivial are those which involve no applications of
H
or
m_{2|1|0}
, i.e. we include via
I
, then apply an action
m_{1|1|0}
,
m_{0|1|1}
of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, and then we apply
\Pi
, as claimed. The second claim about the action of
\mathbb{F}[Z]
is immediate from this description.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

We recall that the bimodules
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
were only well-defined up to homotopy equivalence in
[
5
]
*Proposition 5.10. It follows that the bimodules
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
are well-defined up to homotopy equivalence. Noting, however, that
m_{0|1|0}
vanishes on this module, and that there is at most one generator in a given Maslov-Alexander grading, it follows that the module structure
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is completely specified (not just up to homotopy equivalence).

4.4.
The bimodules
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
and
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}

We observe that the underlying
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
splits canonically as

\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\cong\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\oplus\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}.

Here
\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})
is the
\mathbb{F}
-vector space spanned by
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}Z^{i}
and
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}Z^{i}
, for
i\geq 0
. The module
\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})
is the
\mathbb{F}
-vector space spanned by
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i+1}^{t}Z^{j}
and
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i+1}^{\prime}Z^{j}
for
j\geq 0
. Note that this splitting is canonical, since
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}Z^{i}
and
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}Z^{i}
do not share the same Alexander gradings and idempotents as any other generators of
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
.

Of course, we can also view
\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})
as the quotient of
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
by the subspace
\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})
.

We now consider the interaction of the left
\mathcal{K}
-action with respect to this splitting:

Lemma 4.12
.

The subspace
\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
is a submodule with respect to the bimodule structure over
(\mathcal{K},\mathbb{F}[Z])
.

Proof.

Note that clearly
m_{0|1|1}
preserves
\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})
, so it suffices to check that the left
\mathcal{K}
-action preserves
\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})
. Recall that the only actions on
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
which are non-trivial are
m_{1|1|0}
and
m_{0|1|1}
by Lemma
4.10
. Therefore
m_{1|1|0}(a,-)
commutes with the action of
Z
for all
a\in\mathcal{K}
. Since there are no non-zero maps from a torsion
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module to a free
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module, it follows that
m_{1|1|0}(a,-)
must send
\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})
to itself, proving the claim.
∎

Definition 4.13
.

Suppose that
P
is an L-space satellite operator. Let
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
be the submodule of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
spanned by all of the
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-torsion elements of
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
. Define the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
as the quotient

\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P}):=\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})/\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})

with its induced quotient module structure over
(\mathcal{K},\mathbb{F}[Z])
.

We will see that in many cases, to compute
\tau(P(K,n))
, it is sufficient to consider
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
instead of the full module
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

Remark 4.14
.

In a similar manner to Remark
4.11
, the structure on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is uniquely determined (not just up to homotopy equivalence).

In the above, we are viewing
\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})
as a
(\mathcal{K},\mathbb{F}[Z])
-submodule of
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
. It is helpful to recall that the ordinary notion of a submodule extends to the setting of type-
AA
bimodules in a simple manner. We recall the following:

Definition 4.15
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be two associative algebras over rings
\bm{\mathbf{k}}
and
\bm{\mathbf{j}}
, respectively. Let
{}_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{B}}
be a
AA
-bimodule over
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
. A
(\bm{\mathbf{k}},\bm{\mathbf{j}})
-submodule
N
of
M
is a
sub
AA
-bimodule
of
{}_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{B}}
if

m_{i|1|j}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\otimes i}\otimes N\otimes\mathcal{B}^{\otimes j}\right)\subseteq N,

for all
i,j\geq 0
.

If
N
is a sub
AA
-bimodule of
M
, then the structure maps
m_{i|1|j}
naturally descend to the
(\bm{\mathbf{k}},\bm{\mathbf{j}})
-module quotient
M/N
:

m_{i|1|j}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\otimes i}\otimes(M/N)\otimes\mathcal{B}^{\otimes j}\right)\to M/N.

Furthermore, these maps give an
AA
-bimodule structure on the quotient
M/N
. We call the induced
AA
-bimodule a
quotient
AA
-bimodule
.

Note that the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\subseteq{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
satisfies the above definition of a sub
AA
-bimodule, since it is a submodule by Lemma
4.12
, and
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})
has vanishing higher actions by Lemma
4.10
.

Remark 4.16
.

By the definition of the box tensor product, if
{}_{\mathcal{A}}N_{\mathcal{B}}
is a sub
AA
-bimodule of
{}_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{B}}
, then for any
DA
-bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}L^{\mathcal{A}}
, the box tensor product
{}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}L^{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{A}}N_{\mathcal{B}}
is also a sub
AA
-bimodule of
{}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}L^{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{B}}
. A similar statement holds for quotient
AA
-bimodules. Also, similar remarks hold for tensoring on the left with a type-
D
module
J^{\mathcal{A}}
instead of a
DA
-bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}L^{\mathcal{A}}
.

We now describe the bimodule structure on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
more explicitly:

Lemma 4.17
.

Suppose
P
is an L-space satellite operator, and let
L_{P}
be the associated two-component link. Let
\ell=\mathrm{lk}(\mu,P)
be the linking number of
L_{P}
, and
g=g_{3}(P)
be the Seifert genus of the pattern
P
viewed as a knot in
S^{3}
. Then the left action
L_{a}:=m_{1|1|0}(a,-)\colon\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})\to\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})
of
a\in\{W,Z,\sigma,\tau\}
on the quotient bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
are given by the following formulas for each
t\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{\ell}{2}
:

\displaystyle L_{Z}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{0})=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\text{if $t<\frac{\ell}{2}$,}\\
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t+1}_{0}Z^{j_{t}}&\text{if $t\geq\frac{\ell}{2},$ where $j_{t}=R_{t}-R_{t+1}$. }\end{cases}

\displaystyle L_{W}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{0})=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t-1}_{0}Z^{k_{t}}&\text{if $t\leq\frac{\ell}{2},$ where $k_{t}=R_{t}-R_{t-1}$, }\\
0&\text{if $t>\frac{\ell}{2}$.}\end{cases}

\displaystyle L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{0})=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\text{if $t<\frac{\ell}{2}$,}\\
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}Z^{j^{\prime}_{t}}&\text{if $t\geq\frac{\ell}{2},$ where $j^{\prime}_{t}=R_{t}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)$. }\end{cases}

\displaystyle L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t}_{0})=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}Z^{k^{\prime}_{t}}&\text{if $t\leq\frac{\ell}{2},$ where $k^{\prime}_{t}=R_{t}+\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)$, }\\
0&\text{if $t>\frac{\ell}{2}$.}\end{cases}

Additionally,

L_{T^{\pm 1}}(T^{t}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0})=T^{t\pm 1}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0},\quad\text{and}\quad\L_{U}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0})=0.

Schematically,
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
takes the following form, where we only label the non-zero actions of
L_{a}
for
a=W,Z,\sigma,\tau,T,T^{-1}
, and each generator denotes a free
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module generator:

(4.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-2}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-1}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}+1}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}+2}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-2}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-1}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}+1}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}+2}\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{T^{-1}}}

Proof.

In our construction of the modules
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
, if
\bm{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathcal{C}_{t}\subseteq\mathcal{X}(L_{P})
is a staircase generator in idempotent 0 and in algebraic grading 0, we can construct a model of the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
where
L_{Z}(\bm{\mathbf{x}})=\delta_{2}^{1}(Z,\bm{\mathbf{x}})
is equal to
\bm{\mathbf{y}}\otimes W^{i}Z^{j}
for any choice of staircase generator
\bm{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathcal{C}_{t+1}
which has

(\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}},\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}})(\bm{\mathbf{y}})-(2i,2j)=(\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}},\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}})(\bm{\mathbf{x}})-(2,0).

Similarly, we can pick
L_{W}(\bm{\mathbf{x}})
,
L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}})
and
L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{x}})
to be any staircase generators which have the appropriate gradings. This follows immediately from the description in
[
5
]
*Sections 5.2, 5.3.

Recall from Remarks
4.11
and
4.14
that although the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
is only determined up to homotopy equivalence, the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is completely determined. Therefore we only have to show the stated formulas for some choice of structure map on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
. In the rest of this proof, we will write
\delta_{2}^{1}(a,-)
for the structure maps on the
DA
-bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
, and
L_{a}
for the induced maps on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

We consider first the claim about
L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})
. We break the proof into two cases, depending on whether
t<\ell/2
or
t\geq\ell/2
.

Consider first
t<\ell/2
. We write
\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{i}^{\prime}\otimes W^{p_{t}}Z^{q_{t}}
for some
i
. If
i>0
, there is nothing to show. If
i=0
, then since
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,-))=0
by Equation (
4.1
), we must have

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})-2p_{t}.

We observe
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})=0
since
\mathcal{S}
is s staircase complex with standard gradings in the sense of Definition
4.3
. Furthermore,

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=-2H_{L_{P}}(t,R_{t})=-2H_{L_{P}}(t,\infty)=-2H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)

by Lemma
2.6
and the definition of
R_{t}
. We recall that

H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }t\geq\ell/2\\
\ell/2-t&\text{ if }t<\ell/2.\end{cases}

In particular,
H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)>0
if
t<\ell/2
, and therefore
p_{t}>0
if
t<\ell/2
, so the left action
\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{i}^{\prime}\otimes W^{p_{t}}Z^{q_{t}}
will make no contribution after setting
W=0
.

Next, we consider
t\geq\ell/2
. As before, we write
\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{i}^{\prime}\otimes W^{p_{t}}Z^{q_{t}}
. We claim that we can take
i=0
and
p_{t}=0
. To see this, note that it suffices to show that

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))\leq\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}),

because then a suitable product of
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}
by powers of
Z
will have the correct grading, and can be chosen to be
\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})
in our construction of the module
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
. The grading shift of
\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,-)
is recalled in Equation (
4.1
). We have

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))=-2H_{L_{P}}(t,R_{t})=-2H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)\quad\text{and}\quad\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})=0.

Additionally,

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))=-2H_{L_{P}}(t,R_{t})-2R_{t}+\ell=-2H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)-2R_{t}+\ell\quad\text{and }\quad\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})=-2g.

We note that
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})
because
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})=0
and
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))=-2H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)=0
. On the other hand, the inequality
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\sigma,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))\leq\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})
is equivalent to

(4.8)

0\leq H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)+R_{t}-\ell/2-g.

Since
H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)=0
for
t\geq\ell/2
, Equation (
4.8
) follows from the cases of
\ell/2\leq t\leq N
and
N\leq t
in the statement of Lemma
4.9
. Together, these imply that
R_{t}\geq g+\ell/2
for
t\geq\ell/2
, which yields the formula in Equation (
4.8
).

Next, we easily compute using the above grading formulas in Equation (
4.1
) that
q_{t}=R_{t}+\ell/2-g
for
t\geq\ell/2
.

Next, we address the claim for
L_{\tau}
. As before, we break the claim into two cases, depending on whether
t\leq\ell/2
or
t>\ell/2
.

We consider the case that
t>\ell/2
first. Suppose
\delta_{2}^{1}(\tau,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{i}^{\prime}\otimes W^{p_{t}}Z^{q_{t}}
. If
i>0
, then there is nothing to show, so suppose
i=0
. Using Equation (
4.1
) we compute that

-2p_{t}=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\tau,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))-\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})=-2H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)-2t+\ell.

If
t>\ell/2
, then the right hand of the above equation is
-2t+\ell
, which is negative, so
p_{t}>0
. Therefore
\delta_{2}^{1}(\tau,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})
becomes zero in
\mathcal{X}^{\free}
since we have set
W=0
.

Next, we consider the case that
t\leq\ell/2
. In this case, we claim that we can take
\delta_{2}^{1}(\tau,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}\otimes Z^{q_{t}}
for some
q_{t}\geq 0
. For this to be the case, we need

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\tau,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\tau,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))\leq\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}).

The equation involving the
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-grading is a straightforward computation using Equation (
4.1
), while the inequality involving the
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
-grading is demonstrated as follows.
We compute that

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\delta_{2}^{1}(\tau,\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t}))=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{t})=-2H_{L_{P}}(t,R_{t})-2t-2R_{t}=-2H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)-2t-2R_{t}.

Since
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})=-2g
, it suffices therefore to show

-2H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)-2t-2R_{t}\leq-2g.

Using the value
H_{\mu}(t-\ell/2)=-(t-\ell/2)
for
t\leq\ell/2
, the above inequality is equivalent to

0\leq R_{t}+\ell/2-g,

for
t\leq\ell/2
. This follows from the first two cases of Lemma
4.9
. The formula for
q_{t}
is easily derived using the grading formulas from Equation (
4.1
).

The statements about
L_{W}
and
L_{Z}
follow from a similar line of reasoning, and we leave the details to the reader.
∎

5.
Computation of
\tau(P(K,n))

In this section, we derive our formulas for
\tau(P(K,n))
as stated in Theorem
1.2
. Our main tool is the complex

\mathbb{X}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}:=\mathcal{X}_{n}(S^{3},K)^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]},

which is homotopy equivalent to
\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}(K)
as a type-
A
module over
\mathbb{F}[Z]
. After recalling some basic properties of this complex from
[
5
]
, we study this complex to compute
\tau(P(K,n))
when
\varepsilon(K)=1
and
P
is an L-space pattern. We also compute
\tau(P(K,n))
when
\varepsilon(K)\in\{-1,0\}
under certain extra assumptions on the pattern
P
.

Throughout this section, we orient
P
so that
\ell=\mathrm{lk}(P,\mu)\geq 0
. This does not affect the value of
\tau(P(K,n))
, since this affects neither the value of
\tau(P(K,n))
nor
P
being an L-space pattern.

5.1.
The quotient complex
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}

By Lemma
4.12
,
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\tors}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is a submodule of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, and therefore the quotient
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is naturally a quotient bimodule. By Remark
4.16
, this implies that

\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}:=\mathcal{X}_{n}(S^{3},K)^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}

is a quotient complex of

\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}=\mathcal{X}_{n}(S^{3},K)^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}

over
\mathbb{F}[Z]
. In this section, we describe
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
in more detail.

We begin by recalling from
[
5
]
*Section 9 that the complex
\mathbb{X}(P,K,n)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
decomposed as a 2-dimensional hypercube of chain complexes, taking the following form:

\mathbb{X}(P,K,n)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}=\hbox to144.26pt{\vbox to77.85pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 72.72032pt\lower-39.84204pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

The complex
\mathbb{E}
decomposes as

\mathbb{E}=\bigoplus_{(s,t)\in\mathbb{H}(P)}E_{s,t},

where

\mathbb{H}(P)=\left(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{\mathrm{lk}(P,\mu)+1}{2}\right).

Also
\mathbb{F}
,
\mathbb{J}
,
\mathbb{M}
similarly decompose as direct sums over
\mathbb{H}(P)
of
F_{s,t}
,
J_{s,t}
and
M_{s,t}
. We refer the reader to
[
5
]
*Section 9 for a detailed description of
\mathbb{X}(P,K,n)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
, though we will describe some basic details shortly.

The complex
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)
can similarly be described as a 2-dimensional hypercube of
AA
-bimodules. We will show in Lemma
5.1
that no diagonal differential appears in either
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}
or
\mathbb{X}^{\free}
(i.e. there is no differential from
\mathbb{E}^{\diamond}
to
\mathbb{M}^{\diamond}
).

In
[

]
*Proposition 7.9, we show that the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
has a similar description as a hypercube of
DA
-bimodules. It takes the form

Here,
\mathscr{E}_{*,*}
,
\mathscr{F}_{*,*}
,
\mathscr{J}_{*,*}
and
\mathscr{M}_{*,*}
are supported in (left) idempotents
0
,
0
,
1
and
1
, respectively. The bimodules
\mathscr{E}_{*,*}
,
\mathscr{F}_{*,*}
,
\mathscr{J}_{*,*}
,
\mathscr{M}_{*,*}
are built from the complexes and maps appearing in the bimodule
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
. The subscripts denote the Alexander gradings of the two components of the Hopf link in the connected sum
H\#L_{P}
. In particular, we view each
\mathscr{E}_{s,t}
(and so forth) as being concentrated in Alexander gradings
(s,t)
, where
s
corresponds to the component of
H
in
H\#L_{P}
which is not linked with
P\subseteq L_{P}
, and
t
corresponds to the component of
H
which is linked with
P
.

For each
(s,t)\in\mathbb{H}(P)
,
\mathscr{E}_{s,t}
,
\mathscr{F}_{s,t}
,
\mathscr{J}_{s,t}
and
\mathscr{M}_{s,t}
are finitely generated type-
D
modules over
\mathbb{F}[W,Z]
. Each is equal to one of the complexes
\mathcal{C}_{t^{\prime}}
or
\mathcal{S}
(up to grading shifts).

By performing the construction described in Section
4.3
(i.e. setting
W=0
and then taking homology with respect to
\delta_{1}^{1}
), we can define complexes
\mathscr{E}^{\diamond}_{*,t},\mathscr{F}^{\diamond}_{*,t},\mathscr{J}^{\diamond}_{*,t},\mathscr{M}^{\diamond}_{*,t}
, as well as quotient complexes
\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{*,t},\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{*,t},\mathscr{J}^{\free}_{*,t},\mathscr{M}^{\free}_{*,t}
. The versions labeled free can be viewed as the span over
\mathbb{F}[Z]
of the top generators
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t\pm 1/2}_{0}
in place of
\mathcal{C}_{t\pm 1/2}
and the span of
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}
in place of
\mathcal{S}
. We use the same shifts in
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
- and
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
-gradings as in these complexes.

We can identify
\mathscr{E}_{s,t}^{\free}
as the free
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module

\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{s,t}=\begin{cases}\langle\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t-\frac{1}{2}}_{0}\rangle[Z][2s+1,0]&\text{ if }s<0,\\
\langle\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t+\frac{1}{2}}_{0}\rangle[Z][0,-2s+1]&\text{ if }s>0.\end{cases}

Here,
[x,y]
means an upward shift by
x
in
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-grading, and an upward shift by
y
in the
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
-grading. Also
\langle\bm{\mathbf{x}}\rangle[Z]
means the free
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-module with generator
\bm{\mathbf{x}}
. Denote the unique generator of
\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{s,t}
by
\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s,t}
.

For a fixed
t
, we decompose

\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}(K)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}\boxtimes{}_{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{*,t}=\bigoplus_{s\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}E^{\free}_{s,t},

where
E^{\free}_{s,t}
is the free chain complex over
\mathbb{F}[Z]
where the first coordinate of the Alexander grading equals
s
.

Similarly, we may write

\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{s,t}=\langle\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\rangle[Z][\min(2s+1,0),\min(0,-2s-1)]

for all
s,t
. Denote the unique generator of
\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{s,t}
by
\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s,t}
. Then,
F^{\free}_{s,t}
is the direct summand chain complex with the first Alexander grading equal to
s
of the tensor product

\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}(K)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{*,t}{}.

We also have

\mathscr{J}^{\free}_{s,t}=\langle\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t+\frac{1}{2}}_{0}\rangle[Z]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{M}^{\free}_{s,t}=\langle\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\rangle[Z].

We denote the unique generators of
\mathscr{M}^{\free}_{s,t}
and
\mathscr{J}^{\free}_{s,t}
by
\bm{\mathbf{j}}^{\free}_{s,t}
and
\bm{\mathbf{m}}^{\free}_{s,t}
. The complexes
J^{\free}_{s,t}
and
M^{\free}_{s,t}
are formed in the similar manner as
E^{\free}_{s,t}
and
F^{\free}_{s,t}
, with
\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}(K)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
replaced by the free type-
D
module over
\mathbb{F}[U,T,T^{-1}]
with a single generator
\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}
. Therefore, we have

J^{\free}_{s,t}=\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{j}}^{\free}_{s,t}\rangle[Z]=\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{t+\frac{1}{2}}_{0}\rangle[Z],\quad\text{and}\quad M^{\free}_{s,t}=\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{m}}^{\free}_{s,t}\rangle[Z]=\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\rangle[Z].

The structure maps
\Phi^{\pm K}
and
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
appearing in Equation (
5.1
) are obtained by tensoring the identity on
\mathcal{X}_{n}(K)^{\mathcal{K}}
with the maps
f^{\pm K}
and
f^{\pm\mu}
(respectively) from Equation (
5.2
), i.e.,

\Phi^{K}=\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{n}}\boxtimes f^{K},

and similarly for the other maps. The maps
f^{\pm K}
and
f^{\pm\mu}
are shown in Figures
5.1
and
5.2
, respectively. These are obtained from the maps in
[
5
]
*Section 9 by setting
W=0
and then conjugating with the homotopy equivalence
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\simeq{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
from Lemma
4.10
. In effect, this amounts to taking the description from
[
5
]
*Section 9, setting
W=0
on each type-
D
output, and then deleting any structure maps which increase the algebraic grading. In Figures
5.1
and
5.2
, we summarize the structure maps on
\mathscr{E}_{*,t}^{\diamond}
,
\mathscr{F}_{*,t}^{\diamond},

\mathscr{J}_{*,t}^{\diamond}
and
\mathscr{M}_{*,t}^{\diamond}
, as well as display the morphisms
f^{\pm K}
and
f^{\pm\mu}
. The description on the free versions (
\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{*,t}
and so forth) is schematically the same.

Explicit descriptions of the structure maps
m_{1|1|0}
on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
are described in Lemma
4.17
.

Figure 5.2.
The complexes
\mathscr{E}_{*,t}^{\diamond}
,
\mathscr{F}_{*,t}^{\diamond}
,
\mathscr{J}_{*,t}^{\diamond}
,
\mathscr{M}_{*,t}^{\diamond}
and the maps
f^{\pm\mu}
.

Using the description of
L_{\sigma}
and
L_{\tau}
in Lemma
4.17
, we see that
f^{\mu}:\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{*,t}\to\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{*,t}
is trivial for
t<\frac{\ell}{2}-1
, and
f^{-\mu}:\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{*,t}\to\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{*,t}
is trivial for
t>\frac{\ell}{2}+1
. The behavior is most interesting when
t=(\ell-1)/2
and
t=(\ell+1)/2
.

Lemma 5.1
.

(1)

The module
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
has only
m_{0|1|0}
,
m_{1|1|0}
and
m_{0|1|1}
non-trivial. The same holds for
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

(2)

The hypercube descriptions
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
and
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
(shown in Equation (
5.2
)) have no diagonal differentials.

(3)

The hypercube description of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)
(as in Equation (
5.1
)) have no diagonal differentials.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the fact that
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}
has only
\delta_{1}^{1}
and
\delta_{2}^{1}
non-trivial, and
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
has only
m_{1|1|0}
and
m_{0|1|1}
non-trivial by Lemma
4.10
.

The second claim follows from the first, as follows. Each component of the structure map of the tensor product is obtained by composing the structure maps of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}
. The
\mathcal{K}
outputs are then input into the structure map of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
. For a component of the box tensor product differential to contribute to the diagonal map, we would need a
\sigma
or
\tau
multiple to be input into the structure maps of
\mathcal{H}_{-}
, and then for the composition of structure maps from
\mathcal{H}_{-}
to have a
\sigma
or
\tau
output. By inspection, any such composition involves at least the 2-fold composition of the structure maps of
\mathcal{H}_{-}
. However this pairs trivially with the structure maps of
\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\free}
, since only
m_{1|1|0}
and
m_{0|1|1}
are non-trivial on these modules.

The third claim follows from similar logic as the second.
∎

5.2.
A direct summand of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
when
\varepsilon(K)=1

We now consider the complexes
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
when
P
is an L-space satellite operator and
K
is a knot in
S^{3}
with
\varepsilon(K)=1
. We will describe a direct summand of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
contained in
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, which we call
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
, and which supports the infinite
Z
-tower in the homology. Therefore, it is enough to study this direct summand to compute the
\tau
-invariant of
P(K,n)
in this case.

To construct and analyze this summand, we will use the hat flavored surgery modules
\mathcal{X}_{n}(K)^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
and the structural result of these modules from Theorem
3.5
. As a first step, recall that our module
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}
has
U
-equivariant structure maps, and that the action of any element of
\mathcal{K}
which is a multiple of
U
on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
. Since the action of any
U
multiple vanishes on
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, we may naturally view it as inducing an
AA
-bimodule
{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, whose actions take the same formula. We observe

\begin{split}&{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\\
\cong&{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}\boxtimes{}_{\mathcal{K}}[q_{U}]^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\boxtimes{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\\
\cong&{}_{\mathcal{K}}[q_{U}]^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\boxtimes{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\boxtimes{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}\end{split}

where
{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
(and so forth) are the modules obtained by setting
U
to be zero in the inputs and outputs of
{}_{\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\mathcal{K}}
. Here,
q_{U}\colon\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{K}/(U)=\hat{\mathcal{K}}
denotes the algebra morphism induced by setting
U=0
, and
{}_{\mathcal{K}}[q_{U}]^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
denotes the corresponding
DA
-bimodule (see
[
25
]
*Definition 2.2.48).

In particular, in order to compute
\tau(P(K,n))
, it suffices to work with the hat flavored surgery modules
\mathcal{X}_{n}(K)^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
instead of the full surgery modules
\mathcal{X}_{n}(K)^{\mathcal{K}}
. We will use the description of
\mathcal{X}_{n}(K)^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
in terms of standard complexes, essentially due to Dai, Hom, Stoffregen and Truong, stated above as Theorem
3.5
. According to this theorem, we write

(5.3)

\mathcal{X}_{n}(K)^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}=\mathcal{X}_{n}(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\oplus A.

For the purposes of computing
\tau(P(K,n))
, the summand
A
plays no role. Therefore, for our purposes it suffices to consider the tensor product

\mathcal{X}_{n}(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\boxtimes{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\boxtimes{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{X}^{\diamond}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}.

We recall from Section
3.4
that
\mathcal{X}_{n}(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
is the complex

(5.4)

In the above, if
b_{i}
is positive, there is a differential from
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{i}
to
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{i-1}
(i.e. an arrow to the left). If
b_{i}
is negative, there is a differential from
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{i-1}
to
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{i}
. Note that Hom’s invariant
\varepsilon(K)
is the sign of
b_{1}
if
m>0
. Furthermore,
\varepsilon(K)=0
if and only if
m=0
.

Definition 5.2
.

Define

\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)

to be the free
\mathbb{F}[Z]
-submodule of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
spanned by the following generators:

(1)

\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\in\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}\rangle\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\subseteq E_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\free}
for
s>\tau(K)
;

(2)

\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\in\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}\rangle\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\subseteq F_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\free}
for
s>-1+\tau(K)
;

(3)

\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{j}}^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\in\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\mathscr{J}^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\subseteq J_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\free}
for all
s\in\mathbb{Z}+1/2
;

(4)

\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{m}}^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\in\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\mathscr{M}^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\subseteq M_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\free}
for all
s\in\mathbb{Z}+1/2
;

(5)

\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s+\tau(K),\frac{\ell+1}{2}}\in\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}\rangle\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{s+\tau(K),\frac{\ell+1}{2}}\subseteq E_{s,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}^{\free}
for
s<-\tau(K)
;

(6)

\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s+\tau(K),\frac{\ell+1}{2}}\in\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}\rangle\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{s+\tau(K),\frac{\ell+1}{2}}\subseteq F_{s,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}^{\free}
for
s<-\tau(K)
.

Note that
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
is contained in
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)
. We view
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
as a free chain complex over
\mathbb{F}[Z]
by using all of the components of the differential on
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)
between the generators of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
. These are described in Section
5.1
.

See Figures
5.3
and
5.4
for schematics of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
when
n<2\tau(K)
and
n\geq 2\tau(K)
, respectively. Note that there is a qualitative difference between these two cases, which explains the difference in the formulas of
\tau(P(K,n))
in Theorem
1.2
depending on whether
n<2\tau(K)
or
n\geq 2\tau(K)
.

All the vertical maps are identity maps on
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}
or
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}
. All the leftward pointing arrows labeled by
L_{\tau}
are given by the formula

L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\cdot Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}+\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)}.

All of the rightward arrows labeled by
L_{\sigma}
are given by

L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\cdot Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)}.

These formulas are derived in Lemma
4.17
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle{J^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle{M^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle{M^{\free}_{-\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2}+n,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle{J^{\free}_{-\tau(K)+\frac{1}{2}+n,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle{E^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2}-n,\frac{\ell}{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle{F^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2}-n,\frac{\ell}{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle{F^{\free}_{-\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell}{2}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{-\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{-\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{-\mu}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{-\mu}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{-\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{-K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{-K}}

Figure 5.4.
A schematic drawing of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
when
n\geq 2\tau(K)
, which is obtained from the previous diagram by sliding the bottom row relatively to the right.

Proposition 5.3
.

When
\varepsilon(K)=1
, the complex
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is a direct summand of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

We break the proof of Proposition
5.3
into two lemmas. In Lemma
5.4
, we prove that when
\varepsilon(K)=1
,
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
is a quotient complex. In Lemma
5.5
, we prove that when
\varepsilon(K)=1
,
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
is a subcomplex. Combining these yields Proposition
5.3
.

Lemma 5.4
.

If
K\subseteq S^{3}
is a knot with
\varepsilon(K)=1
, then
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is a quotient complex of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is a quotient complex of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
by Lemma
4.12
, and
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
is a quotient module of
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)
, it suffices to show that
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
is a quotient complex of
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, i.e., there are no non-trivial differentials
m_{0|1|0}
pointing from other generators in
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
to
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
.

Throughout the proof, we will write

C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})

for the standard complex summand of
\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}(K)^{\hat{R}}\simeq C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})\oplus A
for the companion knot
K
, where
b_{1},\dots,b_{m}
are as in Equation (
5.3
). By slight abuse notation, we will assume

\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}(K)^{\hat{R}}=C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m}).

By Lemma
5.1
, there are no length
2
differentials in
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
. Therefore, it is enough to consider length
0
and length 1 differentials. The length 0 differentials are the internal differentials of each
E^{\free}_{s,t},F^{\free}_{s,t},J^{\free}_{s,t},M^{\free}_{s,t}
. The length
1
differentials are the maps
\Phi^{\pm K}
and
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
discussed in Section
5.1
.

We consider the length
0
differentials first. The claim is obvious for
J^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
M^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
, since the only generator in
J^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
is
\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0}
, and similarly for
M^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
, and there are no internal differentials to consider.

For
E^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
with
s>\tau(K)
, by the definition of the standard complex, the only differential in
C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})
pointing to
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}
is

\hbox to82.33pt{\vbox to19.1pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 41.16658pt\lower-6.85417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-41.16658pt}{-1.25pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 16.33 1.73 L -15.77 1.73}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{-11.6pt}{1.25pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-8.08337pt}{3.60277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{

Note
b_{1}
is positive when
\varepsilon(K)=1
. Note also that the Alexander grading of
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}
is
A(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0})=\tau(K)
. From the description of
\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
in Figure
5.1
, we see that the arrow
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}\xleftarrow{W^{b_{1}}}\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{1}
becomes
0
in
E^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
for
s>\tau(K)
.
Therefore, there is no arrow pointing to
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0}
in
E^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
for
s>\tau(K)
. The same observation applies to
F^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
for
s>-1+\tau(K)
by looking at the expression of
\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
in Figure
5.1
.

The corresponding statement about
E^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}
and
F^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}
for
s<-\tau(K)
can be proven by in a similar manner.

For the length
1
differentials (i.e.
\Phi^{\pm K}
and
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
), we begin by considering the subspace of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
in the row consisting of
E^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
F^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
. There are no vertical arrows
\Phi^{\pm K}
pointing into this row, so we only need to consider the horizontal arrows
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
. The maps
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
are described in Section
5.1
. More explicitly, the map

\Phi^{\mu}\colon E^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to F^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}

is given by

\Phi^{\mu}=\mathbb{I}\boxtimes f^{\mu}:C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})\boxtimes\mathscr{E}^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})\boxtimes\mathscr{F}^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}},

where
f^{\mu}
is described in Figure
5.2
. Note that
f^{\mu}
only has one term,
(f^{\mu})_{1}^{1}
, and this acts by
L_{\sigma}
. Therefore the only possible rightward horizontal arrow that can map to
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
in
F^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
is

\mathbb{I}\otimes L_{\sigma}:\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}.

For
s>\tau(K),
we have already included such arrows in the definition of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
. For
s=\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2}
, the corresponding map is

\mathbb{I}\otimes L_{\sigma}:\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-1}_{0}\to\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0},

but
L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-1})=0
in

{}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\mathcal{X}^{\free}(L_{P})_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
by Lemma
4.17
.

For the leftward horizontal arrows, the argument is similar, and this time we have included all the possible non-trivial maps

\mathbb{I}\otimes L_{\tau}:\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K)-1,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}

for
s>\tau(K)
in
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

Again, the statement about the part of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
in the row consisting of
E^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}
and
F^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}
could be obtained similarly.

Finally, let’s consider length-
1
maps pointing to the row consisting of
J^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
M^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
in
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)
. There is nothing to prove for the horizontal maps
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
, as the whole row of
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
consisting of
J^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
M^{\free}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
is contained in
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

The vertical maps
\Phi^{K}:E^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to J^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
are given by
\mathbb{I}\boxtimes f^{K}
, where
f^{K}
is described in Figure
5.1
. If a generator
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{k}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-A(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{k}),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
has a non-trivial arrow of
\Phi^{K}
pointing to
\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{j}}^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
, then
k=0
, since
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}
is the only generator of
C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{n}(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
with a non-trivial
\delta^{1}
whose coefficient is a multiple of
\sigma
.

Therefore, it is enough to consider
\Phi^{K}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{{}^{\free}}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}})
for
s\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}
.

When
s-\tau(K)>0
, the non-trivial arrows are already contained in
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
.

When
s-\tau(K)<-1
, the map
\Phi^{K}
sends
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
to zero because
f^{K}
vanishes on
\mathscr{E}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\free}
by its description in Figure
5.1
.

When
s-\tau(K)=-\tfrac{1}{2}
,
we have

\Phi^{K}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{{}^{\free}}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}})=\Phi^{K}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-1}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|L_{Z}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}-1}_{0})=0,

by Lemma
4.17
.

The proof for
\Phi^{K}:F^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to M^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
is similar. The only difference is this time we allow
s
to be
\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2}
, but that non-trivial arrow is already included in
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
as well.

The proof for
\Phi^{-K}:E^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}\to J^{\free}_{s+n,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
\Phi^{-K}:F^{\free}_{s,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}\to M^{\free}_{s+n,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
is similar, so we leave the details to the reader.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

If
K\subseteq S^{3}
is a knot with
\varepsilon(K)=1
, then
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is a subcomplex of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

Proof.

Unlike in Lemma
5.4
, it is not sufficient to consider only
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, because
\mathbb{X}^{\free}(P,K,n)
is not in general a subcomplex of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)
.

We begin by considering the subspace of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
in the row of
J^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
M^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
first. There are no length-
2
arrows or vertical length-
1
arrows leaving the
J^{\diamond}_{*,*}
and
M^{\diamond}_{*,*}
rows, so we only need to consider the maps
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
(i.e. the maps in a given row).

For the length-
0
maps (the internal differentials of
J^{\diamond}_{s,t}
and
M^{\diamond}_{s,t}
), recall that
J^{\diamond}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
is a copy of
\mathcal{C}^{\diamond}_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}
, and
M^{\diamond}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
is a copy of
\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}
, so there is no differential leaving the top generators
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0}
and
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}
in each of the staircases.

The horizontal length-
1
maps
\Phi^{\mu}:J_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to M_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
\Phi^{-\mu}:J_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\to M_{s-1,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
, are given by

L_{\sigma}:\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}^{\diamond}\to\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}\text{ and }L_{\tau}:\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}^{\diamond}\to\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}

respectively.
It follows from Lemma
4.17
that the
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}
coefficients of

L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}),L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}})\in\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}

are both non-zero. The map
L_{\sigma}
intertwines the
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-grading on
\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}^{\diamond}
with the
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-grading on
\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}
. The map
L_{\tau}
has a more complicated interaction with the gradings, however it is homogeneously graded with respect to
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
and
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
in each fixed Alexander grading. See Equation (
4.1
), and
[
5
]
*Section 5.4 for more details. In particular, the other components of
L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}})
(resp.
L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}})
) must have the same
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-grading as the
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}
component of
L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}})
(resp.
L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}})
). However, all the other generators of
\mathcal{S}
with algebraic grading 0, i.e.,
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{2i}^{\prime}
for
i>0
, have
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-grading strictly smaller than
\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime}
. Therefore, these generators cannot appear as components of
L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}})
or
L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}})
.
This shows that the length-
1
maps in
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
in the
J^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
M^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
will send the top generators to the top generators, as we need. Therefore, there are no differentials leaving the part of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
in the row of
J^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
M^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
.

Consider the part of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)
in the row consisting of
E^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
F^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
. Note that the corresponding statement about the subspace in the row of
E^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}
and
F^{\diamond}_{*,\frac{\ell+1}{2}}
could be obtained similarly. We consider the length-
0
maps first, which are maps within each
E_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
F_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
. Recall, by the description of the standard complex
C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})
, there is no differential leaving
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}
in
C(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})
, so there will be no length-
0
maps in
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
starting at
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
in
E^{\diamond}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
or
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
in
F^{\diamond}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
.

For length-
1
maps, we need to consider both the horizontal maps
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
and the vertical maps
\Phi^{\pm K}
this time. For the horizontal maps,
\Phi^{\pm\mu}
, these are given as tensor products of the form
\mathbb{I}\boxtimes f^{\pm\mu}
. The maps
f^{\pm\mu}
are
AA
-module maps, but are only non-trivial on one input, i.e.
(f^{\pm\mu})_{i|1|j}=0
unless
i=j=0
. In particular, the map
\Phi^{\mu}
is given by
\mathbb{I}\otimes L_{\sigma}
, and similarly for
\Phi^{-\mu}
. See Remark 9.2 in
[
5
]
for the details.

By the restriction on the
s
-coordinate in
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
, we are dealing with

L_{\sigma}:\mathscr{E}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\diamond}\to\mathscr{F}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\diamond}\text{ and }L_{\tau}:\mathscr{E}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\diamond}\to\mathscr{F}_{s-1,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}^{\diamond}

for
s>0,
which corresponds to

L_{\sigma}:\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}^{\diamond}\to\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}\text{ and }L_{\tau}:\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}^{\diamond}\to\mathcal{S}^{\diamond}.

By the same argument as above, we see that these horizontal length-
1
maps between
\mathscr{E}^{\diamond}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
and
\mathscr{F}^{\diamond}_{s,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
for
s>0
will send the top generators to the top generators, as desired.

For the vertical length-
1
maps, note that in the surgery module
\mathcal{X}_{n}(b_{1},\dots,b_{m})^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
, when
b_{1}
is positive, we have

\delta_{1}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}\otimes T^{\tau(K)}\sigma.

Again, by the restriction on the
s
-coordinate and the description of maps
f^{\pm K}
in Figure
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi^{K}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{j}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell}{2}}\otimes 1

\displaystyle\text{and }\Phi^{-K}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{e}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}})=0\,\,

\displaystyle\text{ for }s>\tau(K),

\displaystyle\Phi^{K}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{m}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\otimes 1

\displaystyle\text{and }\Phi^{-K}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{s-\tau(K),\frac{\ell-1}{2}})=0\,\,

\displaystyle\text{ for }s>\tau(K)-1,

and these non-trivial arrows are already included in
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
.

The complex
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)
has no length 2 maps by Lemma
5.1
, so the proof is complete.∎

Combining Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5
, we conclude that
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is a direct summand of
\mathbb{X}^{\diamond}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
as desired.

5.3.
Formulas for
\tau(P(K,n))
when
\varepsilon(K)=1

In this section, we compute
\tau(P(K,n))
when
P
is an L-space satellite operator and
\varepsilon(K)=1
.

Theorem 5.6
.

Suppose
P
is an L-space satellite operator,
K
is a companion knot with
\varepsilon(K)=1
. Let
\ell\geq 0
be the linking number
\ell=\mathrm{lk}(\mu,P)
of the two components of
L_{P}
(which we can assume is nonnegative by choosing the orientation of
P
appropriately). Then, we have the following formula for
\tau(P(K,n))
.

\tau(P(K,n))=\begin{cases}R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}+\frac{(\ell-1)\ell}{2}n+\ell\tau(K),&\text{if $n<2\tau(K),$}\\
g_{3}(P)+\frac{(\ell-1)\ell}{2}n+\ell\tau(K),&\text{if $n\geq 2\tau(K)$. }\end{cases}

In the above,
g_{3}(P)
is the Seifert genus of the pattern
P
viewed as a knot in
S^{3}
, and
R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}
is the second Alexander grading in
\mathbb{H}(L_{P})
of the top generator
\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0}
in the staircase
\mathcal{C}_{\frac{\ell}{2}}
.

Proof.

We divide into two cases depending on whether
n<2\tau(K)
or not.

When
n<2\tau(K)
, we can truncate
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
as drawn in Figure
5.3
, such that it is homotopy equivalent to the following complex:

where the top right element
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
lies in
F^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{l-1}{2}}
, and the bottom left element
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell+1}{2}}
lies in
F^{\free}_{-\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{l+1}{2}}
.
Recall from Lemma
4.17
that the horizontal maps
L_{\sigma}
and
L_{\tau}
are

L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)},\,\,\,L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}+\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)}

respectively, so the homology of
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)^{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
contains an infinite
Z
-tower, which is generated by

\begin{split}&\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)}\\
+&\sum_{j=0}^{2\tau(K)-1-n}\bm{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime}|\bm{\mathbf{j}}^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2}-j,\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\otimes Z^{j\ell}\\
+&\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{m}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell+1}{2}}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}+\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)+\ell(2\tau(K)-1-n)}.\end{split}

Recall we have assumed that
\ell\geq 0
by picking the appropriate orientation on the pattern
P
. The
\tau
invariant of
P(K,n)
is given by the Alexander grading of the the generator of the
Z
-tower, which can be computed from the grading shift formula Equation
(9.5)
in
[
5
]
*Section 9.5. The Alexander grading of

\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)}\in F^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}

is given by

\displaystyle\tau(P(K,n))=

\displaystyle A\big(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A\big(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\big)+R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A^{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}})+\left(\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\ell-1}{2}n\right)\ell+R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0})+\frac{\ell}{2}+\left(\frac{n(\ell-1)-1}{2}+\tau(K)\right)\ell+R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{3}(P)+\frac{(\ell-1)\ell}{2}n+\ell\tau(K)+R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P).

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}+\frac{(\ell-1)\ell}{2}n+\ell\tau(K),

when
n<2\tau(K)
, as desired. Here,
A^{\sigma}
is the
\sigma
-normalized Alexander grading
defined in
[
5
]
*Section 4.4, which satisfies
A^{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}})=A(\bm{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\prime})+\frac{\ell}{2}
in this case.

When
n\geq 2\tau(K)
,
\mathfrak{C}(P,K,n)_{\mathbb{F}[Z]}
is shown in Figure
5.4
. We can truncate the complex to see that it is homotopy equivalent to the following:

where the leftmost copy
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
lies in
F^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
.
Again,the maps
L_{\sigma}
and
L_{\tau}
are the same as before:

L_{\sigma}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}-\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)},\,\,\,L_{\tau}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}_{0})=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}\otimes Z^{R_{\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{2}}}+\frac{\ell}{2}-g_{3}(P)}.

This time, the generator of an infinite
Z
-tower is given by the leftmost copy
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\in F^{\free}_{\tau(K)-\frac{1}{2},\frac{l-1}{2}}
so

\displaystyle\tau(P(K,n))=

\displaystyle A\big(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}|\bm{\mathbf{f}}^{\free}_{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{3}(P)+\frac{(\ell-1)\ell}{2}n+\ell\tau(K),

when
n\geq 2\tau(K)
, as desired.
∎

5.4.
Formulas for
\tau(P(K,n))
when
\varepsilon(K)=0

By
[
8
]
*Remark 3.16,Theorem 6.1,Corollary 6.2, when
\varepsilon(K)=0
, we have

\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}(K)^{\hat{R}}\simeq\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}\rangle\oplus A,

where
\langle\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}\rangle
is the trivial type-
D
module over
\hat{R}
with a single generator
\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0}
. Therefore,
P(K,n)
is locally equivalent to
P(U,n)
, where
U
is the unknot. Hence, we have

\tau(P(K,n))=\tau(P(U,n)).

Thus, when
\varepsilon(K)=0
, it suffices to consider the case when
K
is the unknot, and compute
\tau(P(U,n))
.

The arguments in Lemma
5.4
and Lemma
5.5
do not apply in this case because
\delta^{1}(\bm{\mathbf{y}}_{0})
in
\mathcal{X}_{n}(U)^{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}
contains both
\sigma
-weighted and
\tau
-weighted arrows. However, the complex
\mathbb{X}(P,U,n)^{\mathbb{F}[W,Z]}
is not especially complicated, and we can compute
\tau(P(U,n))
directly.

Lemma 5.7

(
[
5
]
*Section 11.1)
.

Let
U
be the unknot, and

be an L-space satellite operator. Then,
\mathcal{C\hskip-1.42262ptF\hskip-0.85358ptK}(P(U,n))

n
.

(1)

When

,

, viewed as a knot in

, so

,
